## Review Issues (PR 2D round 8 — briar, adversarial read of `f20ec266..529c4db4`, #470)

Scope: clove's two commits closing round 7. Everything before `f20ec266` is out of range.

### Two rows of `GIT_PROBE_CASES` still do not reach their subcommand's work

- **Axis:** `standards`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hook-gate.test.ts` — `GIT_PROBE_CASES.remote`, `GIT_PROBE_CASES.fetch`
- **Problem:** the exit-0 requirement closes the refusal half of round 7's defect but not the other half — a subcommand can exit 0 and still never touch its write path. `remote: ["remote", "-v"]` is the listing spelling, a pure read: run against the probe's own fixture, `.git/config` is byte-identical before and after (md5 `c7e182c1…` both sides). `fetch: ["fetch", "-q", "origin"]` runs against a remote the probe creates one line earlier with `git init --bare` and never seeds, so the transport completes with nothing to transfer — exit 0, a zero-byte `FETCH_HEAD`, no ref written, no object fetched. The set admits `remote` and `fetch` as subcommands that write a remote and a ref; neither row exercises that. Two of fifteen rows, and they are the same two the round-7 finding named alongside `commit` and `push`.
- **Class:** a control that passes because the subject did nothing.
- **Sweep:** walked all fifteen `GIT_PROBE_CASES` rows against what each subcommand's write path actually is. The other thirteen reach it: `commit` commits the dirty victim, `add` stages it, `push` writes `refs/heads/probe` on the bare remote, `tag -a` writes an annotated ref, and the ten inspection rows are reads whose work is reading (`grep line` matches, since the victim's content is `victim line`). All six `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES` rows reach their writes, and `apply`'s and `merge`'s `prepare` scaffolding runs before the snapshot, so it cannot be mistaken for the subcommand's write.
- **Suggested fix:** verified to pass — `remote: { argv: ["remote", "set-url", "origin", "."] }`, and a `prepare` on `fetch` that pushes a ref into the bare remote before the fetch so there is something to transfer. Both together keep the suite green.

### ADR-0072 asserts the property the two rows above do not have

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `.prism/spec/adrs/_toolkit/0072-write-gate-on-routed-paths.md` § Consequences (command axis) and its three mirrors
- **Problem:** the same root cause as the finding above, on the durable surface. The new prose reads "Each subcommand is invoked in a spelling that reaches its own work and is required to exit 0" — and then names `remote` and `fetch` in the very next clause as two of the four the bare form failed. The first half is measured for thirteen rows and asserted for two, and the two it is wrong about are two of the four the sentence cites. Fourth round in which a sentence about the probe states more than the probe does.
- **Class:** reassurance that introduces a new claim (`.prism/rules/writing-voice.md`).
- **Sweep:** read every sentence in the range's new prose that follows an admitted gap — the two new § Consequences paragraphs, the rewritten command-axis paragraph, and the three rewritten JSDoc blocks in `hook.mjs` and `hook-gate.test.ts`. The flag-axis paragraph is still clean. The `everyGitTreeWrite` and `everyTreeSafeGitCommand` JSDoc claims are true as written: the `$(` in the mandated heredoc form is outside `SHELL_READ_SAFE_CHARACTERS`, and `formatShellRerouteMessage` does name `` `git commit -F <file>` ``, so the remedy the prose cites is the one that ships.
- **Suggested fix:** fix the two rows, and the sentence becomes true as written. If the rows are left, scope the sentence to the thirteen it holds for.

### "Membership does not create that exposure" is false for the one shape the paragraph is about

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `.prism/spec/adrs/_toolkit/0072-write-gate-on-routed-paths.md` § Consequences (git-hook paragraph) and its three mirrors
- **Problem:** the paragraph admits that a repo-configured `pre-commit` hook writes routed paths the command text never names, then reassures: "Membership in the tree-safe set does not create that exposure and dropping `commit` would not remove it: a commit whose message names no routed path is outside the gate either way." The subordinate clause is true and the sentence's absolute is not — they cover different shapes. Measured against the shipped arm: `parseUnprovenShellPaths('git commit -m "PRISM-1: fix AGENTS.md"')` returns `["git","-m","m"]` with `commit` on the set and `["git","commit","-m","m","PRISM-1","fix","AGENTS.md"]` with it deleted. For a commit whose text *does* name a routed path — the shape this whole bullet exists for — membership is exactly the difference between the command clearing (and its hooks running) and the command rerouting (and never running). The decision is still right: the alternative is the unsatisfiable deny this ADR exists to prevent. Only the premise carrying it is overstated.
- **Class:** an unqualified universal standing in for a priced tradeoff.
- **Sweep:** ran the counterfactual on the shipped module rather than reasoning from the set. Also checked the reverse direction — with `commit` on the set, no spelling of the other five admitted subcommands gains a hook that reaches tracked content, so the paragraph's scoping of the exposure to `commit` alone holds.
- **Suggested fix:** say what was measured. Membership does not create the exposure for any commit the gate never saw, which is most of them; for the one shape where it does change the outcome, the alternative is a deny with no reachable remedy, and that is the trade being made.

### Minors

- **"The six working-tree writers the set excludes" reads as a closed set, and is not one** (`.prism/spec/adrs/_toolkit/0072-write-gate-on-routed-paths.md` § Consequences and its mirrors; `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs`, `GIT_TREE_SAFE_SUBCOMMANDS` JSDoc). The definite article turns round 7's illustrative list into an enumeration. `git rm`, `mv`, `reset`, `clean`, `pull`, `switch`, `revert`, `rebase`, `cherry-pick`, `am`, `sparse-checkout`, and `worktree` all write the working tree and are all equally absent. The prior wording — "a subcommand that writes the working tree — `checkout`, `restore`, … — is absent" — did not carry the claim. Harm is bounded: the positive probe's `assert.ok(probe, …)` forces a case for any addition and `wrote` catches the tree change, so a reader who widened the set on this reading would still fail the suite. Fix: "six of the working-tree writers", or name them as the negative control's roster rather than as the exclusion's extent.
- **Neither new probe test has a vacuity guard** (`scripts/ai-skills/hook-gate.test.ts`). Both assert only that failure lists are empty, so emptying `GIT_INSPECTION_SUBCOMMANDS` + `GIT_TREE_SAFE_SUBCOMMANDS`, or `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES`, makes each test pass over zero cases. The sibling `SHELL_INSPECTION_COMMANDS` probe carries `assert.ok(clean.length > 0)` for exactly this. Round 7's Sweep noted the missing guard; the fix did not add it. One line each.

### Judged, not filed

- **`-n` off `GIT_TREE_SAFE_FLAGS`: priced right.** The cost is real — `git push -n`, `git add -n`, and `git tag -n` now reroute — but it only bites when the command text also carries a routed path, which is `git add -n <routed path>` and essentially nothing else. `-n` remains on `GIT_INERT_FLAGS`, so `git log -n 5` and `git grep -n` are untouched. The alternative silently clears `git commit -n`, which is `--no-verify`, a spelling `git-conventions.md` forbids and the same JSDoc says it will not widen the proof for. `checkFlagsAreInert` takes one flat set per arm, so there is no partition that keeps both. Correct call, and `everyGitTreeWrite` now pins `git commit -n` as a denial while the generated `everyTreeSafeGitCommand` rows pin `--dry-run` clearing.
- **Declining the `--no-verify` exclusion: agree, on the reasoning as well as the outcome.** Dropping `commit` costs the one shape that had no remedy and closes nothing a token-scan gate can see, and the counterfactual above is the measured version of that. The Major on the sentence is about the sentence, not the decision.
- **Both of clove's claimed mutations reproduce.** Reverting `commit` to the bare argv trips `unproven` with `commit (exit 1: Aborting commit due to empty commit message.)`; softening `stash push` to `stash list` trips the negative control with `stash (exit 0: )`. Run individually, each failing exactly one of the two tests and leaving the other green.

### Angle Coverage — PR 2D round 8, `f20ec266..529c4db4`

- **Correctness / logic** — `swept`; `probeGitSubcommand`'s nested temp-repo and ordering (seed → `prepare` → dirty → snapshot → run), all fifteen `GIT_PROBE_CASES` rows, all six `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES` rows, the `-n` removal from `GIT_TREE_SAFE_FLAGS`, the `doctor.ts` JSDoc move, the `verify-manifest-coverage.ts` `Manifest` de-duplication. One finding.
- **Types** — `swept`; `GitProbeCase`, `probeGitSubcommand`'s return shape, and the `import { type Manifest }` / `export type { Manifest }` pair. Clean — `pnpm prism:check` exit 0 covers it.
- **Tests** — `swept`; both new tests, their two claimed mutations re-run individually, and the two new `everyGitTreeWrite` rows. Two findings.
- **Security / injection** — `swept`; the flag-set change walked for what it newly admits (nothing) and newly rejects (`-n` on the tree-safe arm only), and the `git commit` hook exposure re-measured against the shipped module. One finding.
- **Performance** — `n/a`; the probe adds a second temp repo per case and the suite runs in ~5s.
- **Accessibility** — `n/a`; no UI in range.
- **Spec and doc consistency** — `swept`; ADR-0072's four rewritten passages across all four copies (byte-identical), the three rewritten JSDoc blocks, and the plan's own round-7 entries. Two findings and one minor.
- **Citation integrity** — `swept`; `git-conventions.md` § Formatting against the heredoc claim, `SHELL_READ_SAFE_CHARACTERS` against the `$(`-is-outside-the-class claim, and `formatShellRerouteMessage` against the `-F`-is-the-named-remedy claim. All three hold.
- **Docs impact** — `swept`; grepped `.prism/architect`, `.prism/spec`, `.prism/rules`, and `AGENTS.md` for the flag roster and the tree-safe set. Only ADR-0072 describes them; `install-layout.md` § Write gate describes the gate's shape, which the `-n` change does not alter. No update owed.

## Review Issues (PR 2D round 9 — briar, adversarial read of `529c4db4..22f98196`, #470)

Scope: clove's two commits closing round 8. Everything before `529c4db4` is out of range.

**No issues found — 2026-08-20 [huntermcgrew/opus5-port-deny-gate].** Round 8's three Majors and two Minors are all closed, and the repair holds against checks the repair itself did not run.

### What the pass verified

- **The `confirmWork` read-back reads each row's own write, not a state true regardless.** Four mutations, each tripping `unproven` with `exit 0, but its write never landed` and each leaving the negative-control arm green: restoring `remote -v`; deleting `fetch`'s `prepare`; `fetch --dry-run` with `prepare` left in place; and `remote get-url` in place of `set-url`. The third is the one that matters — `prepare` ran, the branch existed in the bare remote, and the read-back still failed, because `refs/remotes/origin/probe-fetch` lands in the probe repo only when the fetch itself writes it. The fourth is its `remote` twin: a spelling that only reads leaves `get-url` at the bare-remote path.
- **The class is closed, not just the two instances.** Walked all fifteen `GIT_PROBE_CASES` rows against the failure mode — exit 0 without reaching the write path. The nine inspection rows have no write to read back, so `confirmWork` is meaningless for them by construction. The four remaining tree-safe rows reach their writes structurally rather than incidentally: `commit` exits 1 with nothing to commit, `push` and `tag` run against a bare remote and a repo created fresh per probe, and `add` stages the victim the harness dirties two lines earlier. Leaving `confirmWork` optional with `?? true` is the right shape — requiring it everywhere would demand a read-back from nine rows that write nothing.
- **Both vacuity guards are themselves non-vacuous.** `proven` is pushed only on a row that ran, exited 0, confirmed its work, and left the tree clean, so emptying the two subcommand sets trips it; `caught` is pushed only on a writer seen changing the tree, so emptying `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES` trips it. Assertion order puts the more specific failure first in both arms.
- **The ADR's git-hook paragraph rests on something checkable.** `checkSegmentWritesNoFile` returns true for a `git commit` with inert flags and false without `commit` on the set, which is the clearing-versus-rerouting difference the new sentence claims. The bound it names holds on both spellings that skip hooks: neither `--no-verify` nor its short form `-n` is on `GIT_TREE_SAFE_FLAGS`, and `-n` stays on `GIT_INERT_FLAGS` only, where it cannot reach a tree-safe subcommand.
- **The "six writers" sweep is complete.** No `six working-tree writers` phrasing survives anywhere in the tree outside this plan's own history. The three ADR mirrors are byte-identical to `.prism/`.

### Judged, not filed

- **A future row added without `confirmWork` gets no mechanical nudge toward one.** The type defaults to `true`, so a new tree-safe subcommand with an exit-0-idle spelling would pass the way `remote` and `fetch` did. Not a finding: the JSDoc now states the criterion in the place an author adding a row reads, and the alternative — a required field — is wrong for the nine rows that write nothing. This is the same residual shape as the flag axis, which ADR-0072 § Consequences already admits and prices.

### Angle Coverage

Standards axis:

- **Runtime behavior** — `swept` — 5 items enumerated, 5 verdicts. `probeGitSubcommand` (now returns `workConfirmed`, evaluated after the tree snapshot) — correct; `GIT_PROBE_CASES.remote` (argv moves from a listing to a write) — correct; `GIT_PROBE_CASES.fetch` (gains `prepare`, which writes only into the bare remote and a throwaway seed repo, never into `dir`) — correct; `seedRemoteBranch` (asserts each of its four setup commands, so a silent setup failure surfaces as a setup message rather than as a probe verdict) — correct; the tree-safe arm's classification branch (`unproven` on unconfirmed work, then the `changed ? wrote : proven` split, so a writer never counts as proof of a clean pass) — correct.
- **Test efficacy** — `swept` — 4 items enumerated, 4 verdicts. `remote` reaching its write — fails under both `remote -v` and `remote get-url`; `fetch` reaching its write — fails under both a deleted `prepare` and `fetch --dry-run` with `prepare` intact; an emptied positive set — fails `proven.length > 0`; an emptied writer table — fails `caught.length > 0`.
- **External-system claims** — `swept` — 5 items enumerated, 5 verdicts. `git remote set-url`, `git remote get-url`, `git rev-parse --verify -q <ref>`, `git fetch`'s default `+refs/heads/*:refs/remotes/origin/*` refspec written by `git remote add`, and `git push <url> HEAD:refs/heads/<name>` — each verified by running it in the probe rather than from memory, and the refspec claim specifically by the `--dry-run` mutation, which shows the tracking ref appearing only when the fetch itself runs.
- **Repo writing rules** — `swept` — verdict-only. Every new JSDoc block leads with purpose and carries its reason; no tags, no ALL CAPS, no changelog voice. The two new assertion messages name the contract under test rather than the change that added them.
- **Security** — `swept` — 1 item enumerated, 1 verdict. The one trust boundary in range is `GIT_TREE_SAFE_SUBCOMMANDS`'s hook exposure, whose membership the diff leaves untouched and whose bound the ADR now states: neither `--no-verify` nor `-n` is on `GIT_TREE_SAFE_FLAGS`, and `-n` reaches only `GIT_INERT_FLAGS`, so no admitted spelling skips the hooks.
- **Accessibility** — `n/a` — no UI in the pinned range.

Spec axis:

- **Spec and doc consistency** — `swept` — 7 items enumerated, 7 verdicts. ADR-0072 § Consequences command-axis paragraph and git-hook paragraph — both now describe what the code does; the three ADR mirrors — byte-identical to `.prism/`; `GIT_TREE_SAFE_SUBCOMMANDS` JSDoc, `GitProbeCase` JSDoc, and `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES` JSDoc — each matches the table it documents; the plan's round-8 `## Review Issues` statuses — all five moved to `fixed`, matching the shipped code. No acceptance criterion is in range; AC-25 stays ungraded and out of this lane.
- **Citation integrity** — `swept` — 6 items enumerated, 6 verdicts. The ADR's `git remote -v` and idle-`git fetch` claims — both reproduced; its "those two rows" count — matches the two rows carrying `confirmWork`; hook.mjs's "Six of them" — matches `GIT_TREE_WRITER_CASES`'s six entries; the six writer names in both prose homes — identical; `hook-gate.test.ts` as the file running the probe — correct.
- **Docs impact** — `n/a` — no feature, component, or module with a matching docs file changed; the ADR is itself the doc surface and is in the diff, so it is covered under Spec and doc consistency rather than here.

## Review Issues (PR 2D round 11 — briar, adversarial read of `d34755b8..8fcb2b03`, #470)

Scope: the Windows separator fix — `normalizeShellSeparators` on both shell arms, `:` in `PATH_SHAPED_RUN` with its recovery clause, the probe's config isolation, four suite rows, and the ADR/architect-doc prose. Everything before `d34755b8` is out of range.

Two Majors, both confirmed by running the parsers at `d34755b8` and at `8fcb2b03` and diffing the answers. Both are regressions this range introduces, and each runs in the direction its own arm calls fatal.

### The `:` recovery strips a prefix only, so a routed path followed by `:` stops being a candidate

- **Axis:** `standards`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs:735`
- **Problem:** `run.replace(/^[^/:]*:[-+]*/, "")` recovers a path only when the colon precedes every `/` in the run, so a routed path carrying a trailing `:<anything>`, or two routed paths in a colon-separated list, now yields nothing but the glued token and the gate never fires on it.
- **Evidence:** `tee .prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md:s` yields `[".prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md", "s"]` at `d34755b8` and `[".prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md:s"]` at `8fcb2b03`. Same regression on `tee .prism/rules/a.md:.prism/rules/b.md` (both routed paths lost) and `sed -i s/a/b/ .prism/rules/a.md:orig`. Run against both trees, not reasoned.
- **Class:** a token-recovery clause written against the two spellings that broke the suite, rather than against the shape of the character that was admitted.
- **Sweep:** `grep -rn 'PATH_SHAPED_RUN' scripts/` — one consumer, `scanPathShapedTokens`, whose recovery block has two arms. Arm 1 (`^[-+]+` strip) — clean, unaffected by the `:` admission. Arm 2 (the new `:` strip) — this finding. No third arm.
- **Suggested fix:** replace the `unqualified` clause with a split on `:`, adding each piece with the leading-`[-+]` strip already applied. That is a strict superset of both current recoveries — `C:/repo/x.ts` still yields `/repo/x.ts`, `OUT:-src/x.ts` still yields `src/x.ts` — and it recovers the routed path from both broken shapes. It removes a special case rather than adding one.
- **Test gap it rides:** the two shapes the `:` admission broke are pinned (the drive row, the pre-existing `${OUT:-…}` row); the shape it newly broke is not. A row asserting a routed path carrying a colon stays a candidate is what would have caught this.

### The read arm credits a routed doc a POSIX shell never opened

- **Axis:** `standards`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs:179`
- **Problem:** normalizing before the class test makes `cat .prism\architect\_toolkit\install-layout.md` return `{ credit: true }` on the routed doc. On macOS, Linux, and Git Bash the shell reads `.prismarchitect_toolkitinstall-layout.md`, `cat` fails, and nothing is delivered — but `runPostToolUseArm` never inspects `tool_response`, so credit banks on the observed call and the write gate opens on every path routing to that doc.
- **Evidence:** the same command returns `[]` at `d34755b8` and `[{"filePath":".prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md","credit":true}]` at `8fcb2b03`.
- **Why this is not the disclosed residual re-filed:** ADR-0072 prices the fourth gap as needing a spelling nobody writes **and** a doc at the rewritten path. For the gate's own docs the second clause is free — the rewritten path *is* the routed doc, picked by whoever writes the command, and the deny message hands the model that exact path to `cat`. One of the two stated preconditions is satisfied by construction rather than by coincidence, which is the cheaper-reachability bar this round was asked to apply.
- **Class:** a normalization added to close an under-deny on one arm, applied unchanged to the arm where the same rewrite produces an over-credit.
- **Sweep:** `grep -n 'normalizeShellSeparators' scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs` — two call sites. `parseUnprovenShellPaths:877` (write arm) — correct, this is the bypass the PR closes. `parseShellReadTargets:179` (read arm) — this finding. No third.
- **Suggested fix:** keep the rewrite on both arms, and drop credit for any read target whose raw operand carried a rewritten `\` — announce it, never credit it. A Windows user re-cats with the forward-slash spelling the deny message already prints; the cost is one re-read, which is the trade this file states everywhere else ("under-crediting costs one re-read while over-crediting silently defeats the write gate this channel feeds").

### The new test JSDoc claims three CI legs; the workflow has two

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `minor`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hook-gate.test.ts:2772`
- **Problem:** the block says "on macOS and ubuntu they exercise the forward-slash spelling" and "These rows put the backslash reading on all three legs," but `.github/workflows/prism-check.yml`'s matrix is `[ubuntu-latest, windows-latest]` — there is no macOS leg. The same range's `install-layout.md:161` says "both CI legs," so the diff contradicts itself.
- **Class:** a durable comment asserting a CI topology the workflow does not have.
- **Sweep:** `grep -rn 'macos\|macOS\|three legs\|both legs\|CI leg'` over the range's files plus the workflow — one in-range wrong instance (this one), one pre-existing wrong instance out of range (`hook-gate.test.ts:590`, "Both other CI legs run it", same belief, not this diff's to fix), and two in-range correct (`install-layout.md` "both CI legs", ADR-0072 "only the Windows CI leg").
- **Suggested fix:** say "both CI legs" and drop macOS, or say "every leg that runs the suite" if local macOS runs are meant. The substantive claim — the rows run on a majority leg rather than only Windows — holds either way.

### A Decision sentence is refuted by its own residual bullet

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `minor`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `.prism/plans/opus5-port.md:324`
- **Problem:** "`cat foo\ bar.md` … keeps returning zero targets — the same answer it gives today, so the POSIX case gains no over-credit." The escaped-space instance is safe and verified; the generalization to "the POSIX case" is refuted by the Residual-risk bullet six lines down, which states the POSIX no-op escape does credit. This clears the contradicts-the-diff bar rather than being bookkeeping: the code credits `foo/bar.md`, and this is the sentence a future hardener reads before deciding whether POSIX needs looking at.
- **Class:** reassurance that widens an admission into a claim the same passage refutes — `.prism/rules/writing-voice.md` § Anti-pattern: Reassurance that introduces a new claim.
- **Sweep:** read the sentence after each admission in the Decision. The other three admissions (`splitShellSegments` measured rather than reasoned; the hardened surface untouched; the seven red rows) each close on the admission or on something re-verified. One instance.
- **Suggested fix:** scope the clause to what was measured — "so the escaped-space spelling gains no over-credit" — and let the Residual-risk bullet carry the rest.

### Checked and cleared — not findings

- **Escape preservation holds.** `cat docs/one\ two.md`, `cat foo\"bar.md`, `cat foo\$bar.md`, `tee \<newline>` and its CR twin all still yield zero targets or stay unproven. Clove's claim that the escaped-space case is "pinned by the existing allow-list row" is true — `hook-gate.test.ts`'s allow-list row carries `"cat docs/one\\ two.md"`, and widening the lookahead to admit a space would fail it. The discriminator's safety property is tested where it matters.
- **The `typeof` guard is correct and load-bearing.** Clove disclosed it as behavior-identical, which is right about the outcome; it is also now required, since the normalizer runs before both already-guarded callees and `undefined.replace` would throw. No change needed.
- **`splitShellSegments`'s unreachable-branch claim survives.** Every `\` that survives normalization is escape-shaped and still fails `SHELL_READ_SAFE_CHARACTERS`, so the heredoc branches, the `&`/`|` cut, and the double-quote backslash branch stay unreachable exactly as documented. The stated reason still holds, not just the conclusion.
- **Drive-letter recovery works.** `tee C:\repo\src\index.ts` yields both `C:/repo/src/index.ts` and `/repo/src/index.ts`, so neither reading is lost.
- **Mirror parity holds.** ADR-0072 is byte-identical across `.claude`, `.codex`, `.cursor`, `.prism`. `install-layout.md` differs between `.prism` and the three only on pre-existing token-substitution lines; the line this range changed is identical in all four, and no "Three gaps survive" phrasing survives anywhere.
- **No stale separator prose elsewhere.** `templates/install/.prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md` and `.prism/rules/context-reuse.md` state no separator behavior, so clove's decision to leave them alone is right.

## Review Issues (PR 2D round 12 — briar, adversarial read of `e35c4bac..5c63aee7`, #470)

Both round-11 Majors and both Minors are closed, verified against the code rather than taken from the report. One Major and two Minors open, none of them a behavior defect — the range's runtime behavior is correct on every shape I could construct.

### The whole-command scope of the credit refusal is not pinned by any test

- **Axis:** `standards`
- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hook-gate.test.ts:2779`
- **Problem:** both rows that exercise the new credit refusal put the rewritten `\` inside the credited operand itself, where whole-command and per-operand suppression agree. Nothing pins the choice the JSDoc argues for, so a later simplification to per-operand reinstates exactly the round-11 Major on a second operand or a second segment.
- **Evidence:** replacing `const rewritten = normalized !== command; … credit: target.credit && !rewritten` with the per-operand `credit: target.credit && command.includes(target.filePath)` leaves `pnpm prism:test` at 829/829, 0 fail. Mutation applied and reverted in this worktree; the tree is back at `5c63aee7`. The sibling property one function up is pinned: `hook-gate.test.ts:803` asserts `cat docs/one.md\ngrep alpha docs/two.md` yields `[]`, which a per-segment class test would fail.
- **Class:** a deliberately whole-command rule whose only tests place the trigger inside the operand being judged, so the per-operand implementation it rejects is indistinguishable from it.
- **Sweep:** `grep -n 'test("parseShellReadTargets' scripts/ai-skills/hook-gate.test.ts` — four rows plus the two added this round; none carries a second operand or a second segment. The paired write arm was swept the same way and needs nothing: `parseUnprovenShellPaths` over-denies by design, so it has no credit to withhold.
- **Suggested fix:** one row — `parseShellReadTargets("cat <routed-doc> other\\x.md")` asserting `credit: false` on the routed doc. A `;`-separated second segment covers the same property and reads closer to the JSDoc's own example.
- **Fixed in:** `ee072e0c` — one row, `parseShellReadTargets: a rewrite outside the credited operand still costs credit`, carrying all three positions rather than one: a sibling operand of the same `cat`, a later `;` segment, and an earlier newline segment. Control checked by re-running briar's mutation — `credit: target.credit && command.includes(target.filePath)` returns 83/84 with only this row failing, and the tree is back at `!rewritten`, confirmed by grep.

### The JSDoc's governed-shape enumeration is still narrower than the rule

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `minor`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs:186`
- **Problem:** the paragraph names "a second operand of the same `cat`, or an earlier newline-separated segment" as the shapes whole-command suppression governs. Six more positions govern identically, so the second attempt at this enumeration is narrower than the rule for the same reason the first was wrong.
- **Evidence:** probed against the real exported `parseShellReadTargets`, all returning `credit: false` on the routed doc where per-operand would credit it — a *later* newline segment; an earlier `;` segment; a later `;` segment; a `;` segment naming a non-read command (`ls a\b; cat <doc>`); a rewritten segment head token (`l\s x; cat <doc>`); and a rewrite inside a quoted operand. `;` is in `SHELL_READ_SAFE_CHARACTERS` (`hook.mjs:96`) and `splitShellSegments` (`hook.mjs:300`) ends a segment on it, so the newline framing excludes a separator the class admits.
- **Class:** a comment justifying a general rule by enumerating instances, where the enumeration is not closed and the general property is one sentence.
- **Sweep:** `grep -n 'char === ";"' scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs` plus reading `SHELL_READ_SAFE_CHARACTERS` — `&` and `|` are refused by the class, so newline, `\r`, and `;` are the whole separator set; the enumeration omits two of the three. The sibling whole-command paragraph on the class test states its property without enumerating and needs no change.
- **Suggested fix:** replace the enumeration with the property — the rewrite can land anywhere in the command text, and only a whole-command test sees a `\` that is not in the operand being credited — then keep one example rather than a list that has now been wrong twice.
- **Fixed in:** `ee072e0c` — the paragraph now states that a rewrite is a property of the command text rather than of any one operand, keeps `cat <doc> other\x.md` as its single example, and defers the separator set to `splitShellSegments` instead of naming members of it. The duplicated heredoc sentence came out; the same point is already made fifteen lines above in the same JSDoc.

### The split's recorded safety rests on a per-shape claim that does not hold for the whole loss class

- **Axis:** `spec`
- **Severity:** `minor`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs:740`
- **Problem:** the acceptance recorded for dropping the `unqualified` clause is that the two lost tokens match no route and need a spelling a space or `=` would break. Measured over 22 shapes, ten lose a token and three of those tokens are routed paths, so the stated reason is false for the class even though the verdict is right. Neither the comment nor the plan states the property that actually makes the losses safe, so the next editor of the split has to re-derive it and would re-derive it per-shape.
- **Evidence:** `cp -C:/repo/.prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md /tmp/x` loses the routed `C:/repo/.prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md`; so do `tee ${OUT:-C:/repo/.prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md}` and its `:+` twin, neither of which a space or an `=` breaks. The losses are nonetheless harmless, and generally so: every lost token is a drive-qualified spelling, `filterRoutedPaths` reaches a route only through `toRepoRelativePath`, which returns `null` for anything outside `repoRoot`, and `repoRoot` is `findRepoRoot(cwd)` and so always on cwd's drive. Run, not recalled — `path.win32.resolve("C:\\repo\\sub", "/repo/.prism/architect/x.md")` and `path.win32.resolve("C:\\repo\\sub", "C:/repo/.prism/architect/x.md")` both give `C:\repo\.prism\architect\x.md`, so the surviving driveless twin resolves identically; cross-drive, `path.win32.relative("D:\\repo", "C:\\repo\\x")` returns an absolute path and the candidate is dropped.
- **Class:** an acceptance justified by enumerating the instances that are safe rather than by the invariant that makes them safe, so the justification stops holding the moment the instance set grows.
- **Sweep:** `node` harness comparing the pre-change and post-change bodies of `scanPathShapedTokens` over 22 commands — 10 lose a token, 0 lose a token that can reach a route; `grep -rn "unqualified" scripts/` — none found, the old clause is gone cleanly.
- **Fixed in:** `ee072e0c` — the comment now carries the bound rather than a list, and the bound is wider than the suggested fix. Re-running the same old-versus-new comparison over 17 shapes found a loss class outside "always a drive-qualified spelling": `tee -.prism/architect/_toolkit/install-layout.md:x` drops a routed token with no drive letter, so that sentence would have been the third under-general list in one comment. What holds for every shape is that a dropped token always keeps a `:` inside it, and every colon-piece of it plus the raw run stay candidates — so the drop hides a route only where the routed path spans the `:` itself, which on Windows is the drive prefix. The comment then closes the drive case on briar's own executed chain: `repoRoot` is `findRepoRoot(cwd)` and so shares cwd's drive, the driveless twin is a surviving piece that `path.win32.resolve` sends to the identical file, and a foreign-drive path is outside `repoRoot` where `toRepoRelativePath` returns `null`. Both `path.win32` results re-executed here.
- **Suggested fix:** one sentence in the `scanPathShapedTokens` comment — a colon-piece the split drops is always a drive-qualified spelling, and its driveless twin resolves to the same path whenever `toRepoRelativePath` can match a route at all, because `repoRoot` shares cwd's drive.

## Review Issues (PR 2D round 14 — briar, fresh-model read of `b3ffee35..73caf734`, #470)

### The split-loss comment's safety closure covers only the drive-prefix case the matcher does not limit it to

- **Severity:** `major`
- **Status:** `fixed`
- **File:** `scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs:755`
- **Problem:** the comment's closure — "the drop can hide a route only where the routed path spans the `:` itself — on Windows, the drive prefix. That prefix is not needed here" — treats the drive prefix as the only colon-spanning routed path, but the route matcher admits `:` in both wildcard forms (`*` compiles to `[^/]*`, `**` to `.*`), so a POSIX colon-named file under a suffix-anchored glob is a colon-spanning routed path with no drive letter, and it is demonstrably hidden from every surviving candidate.
- **Evidence:** executed at `73caf734`: `compileMatcher("src/*.ts")("src/a:b.ts")` is `true`; `parseUnprovenShellPaths("tee ${OUT:-src/a:b.ts}")` yields `["tee","OUT:-src/a:b.ts","OUT","src/a","b.ts"]` and no candidate matches the route; the flag-glued spelling `tee -src/a:b.ts` hides it the same way; the pre-split body's `unqualified` reading (`run.replace(/^[^/:]*:[-+]*/, "")`, at `870eac91~1`) recovered `src/a:b.ts` and matches. The bound's first half is verified true by construction — a colonless reading is always the raw run or a single piece, both candidates — and both harnesses' "0 routed losses" is true against this repo's manifest, whose patterns are all exact, dir-prefix, or prefix-`**`, shapes where the first colon-piece always rescues the route. The hook ships to consumer repos (`scripts/ai-skills/hooks/**` is in the mirrored ship surface), whose manifests can carry suffix-anchored globs.
- **Class:** a safety closure argued over one instantiation of the property's trigger condition (the Windows drive prefix) and presented as covering the class (any colon-spanning routed path) — the fourth under-general justification in this arm's series, in the commit that fixed the third.
- **Sweep:** node repro against the real exports (above); `grep -n "drive" scripts/ai-skills/hooks/hook.mjs` — every drive-closure sentence in the file sits in this one comment block (743–763), so the class has no second in-range instance in code; the same sentence's two plan copies (the round-13 Close bullet and the third round-12 entry's Fixed-in line) carry the same narrowing and are sites of this finding, not separate findings. The sibling property comment (`parseShellReadTargets`'s whole-command JSDoc) was attacked separately and holds — `rewritten = normalized !== command` cannot leak credit past a rewrite, and its own closure ("the remedy is forward-slash on every platform") was verified against `formatDenyMessage` composing `ARCHITECT_DIR_PREFIX` (`".prism/architect/"`).
- **Suggested fix:** two honest shapes — clove's call which. (a) Comment-only: state the residual as an accepted loss — the drop can also hide a colon-named routed file under a suffix-anchored glob, impossible on Windows and absent from this repo's manifest, accepted as out of scope for a separator fix. (b) Code: also add the two whole-run readings the pre-split body carried (leading `[-+]+` stripped off the whole run; everything through the first `:` plus `[-+]*` stripped), which closes both demonstrated spellings at no cost to any existing row, with one row pinning `${OUT:-src/a:b.ts}` — noting (b) still leaves interior-colon joins (`src/a:b.ts:bak`) unrecovered, which the pre-split body also never recovered, so the comment states a bound either way.
- **Fixed in:** `7b24006f` — took shape (b), because this arm's premise is refuse-unless-provable and an under-deny residual is the direction it calls fatal. Generalized rather than restored literally: the old `unqualified` regex only fired when the colon preceded every slash, and that condition is the under-recovery `870eac91` was fixing, so it comes back as "everything from this boundary to the end of the run" with no slash condition. Each colon is now read both ways from every boundary — the piece to the next colon, the tail to the end of the run — which subsumes both dropped whole-run readings in one rule and stays linear in the run's colon count. Two rows added: `${OUT:-src/nested/a:b.ts}` recovering `src/nested/a:b.ts`, and `` -C:\repo\src\index.ts `` recovering `C:/repo/src/index.ts`. Both fail at `48fd3fb3` and pass here (84/86 → 86/86 on the file; suite 830 → 832), and no other row moved. The drive-closure paragraph is deleted rather than reworded: the tail reading recovers the drive spelling, so the argument it existed to make has nothing left to defend.
